tbmm-2017-06-15sit02spe0141par0001	I now open the Second Session of the 107th Union of Turkey's Grand National Assembly. Congressmans, I'd like to share my opinion with the General Assembly on a matter on the agenda. Turkey's Grand National Assembly and its lawmakers are representatives of the political views that exist in society within the constitutional borders. Lawmakers who are authorized to make decisions on behalf of the public by free elections serve in Parliament as their primary roles. The fact that their primary position is Parliament gives this task a superior public quality. In the 67th article of our Constitution, the authority to represent is also established. Lawmakers use the authority they receive from the public under Article 67 of the Constitution. The main use of this authority is Parliament, the Grand National Assembly of Turkey. Here's the basic rule of law not to interfere with this representational authority, not to interfere with it. As a matter of fact, Article 83 of our Constitution, under the title of the immunity of the law, the immunity of the law and the irresponsibility of the law. In this framework, no deputy can be detained, questioned, arrested, or tried without the permission of the Turkish Grand National Assembly, and that is the main rule. There's no need to touch the essence of representation. However, by a law adopted by the Turkish Grand National Assembly in 2016, the path of trial has been opened for the proceedings against MPs as of a specific date. So the permission of the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, mentioned in Article 83 of the Constitution, has been granted. So in this framework, the trial of lawmakers whose rights have been set up on a specific date is a natural consequence of the adopted law, the Constitutional amendment. But here comes the Supreme Court's Mustafa Balbay and Mehmet Haberal decrees again. These decisions show that the authority to represent is from the 67th Article of the Constitution, saying that no practice should undermine its authority, its essence, and it should not eliminate it. So, according to this, lawmakers can be tried and convicted as a result of this trial on the MP, but the lawmakers can never be arrested, that's the main rule. In the event of finalisation of the ruling, the end of the parliamentary mission is also arranged in the relevant articles of the Constitution. That's true of all lawmakers. In a very brief way, at the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, dated December 8, 2016, I had a need to express it from this bench, and today I feel the need to repeat it again. There is a public interest to be achieved by arrest, but the public benefit, which comes from the authority of representation, is named in law as the superior public interest, and it stands in the way of other public benefits. Therefore, the absence of arrest of lawmakers is essential. The parliamentary immunity is not a privilege; it is a means to fulfil the authority of the law, free and independent, without any pressure. Therefore, it is not necessary to consider it a privilege. Arresting must be an exceptional way for all citizens, so no matter who the subject of arrest is, you have to stick to the law, I'll tell you that. Finally, I will say this: Turkey's Grand National Assembly is the highest office in which our society is represented by all its colors, its opinions, its national will. As the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, all members here, all MPs, I'm sure we all have a goal to achieve a peaceful and peaceful society ideal, and I don't think any of us feel differently about it. To achieve that ideal, now is the time to embrace each other, to embrace each other, and to walk together into the light future in this air of peace and freedom. I feel the need to share these wishes with the General Assembly, thank you for listening to me. [[Applause from CHP and HDP rows]] We're going on the agenda. [[Diyarbakır]] [[Diyarbakır]] 3) Filiz Kerestecioğlu Demir [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İstanbul]] [[İzmir]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mardin]] [[Mwah.]] [[Mwah.]] [[Siirt]] [[Siirt]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şırnak]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Şanlıurfa]] [[Tunceli]] [[Van]] 23) Tuğba Hezer Özturk [[Van]] Diyarbakir, who has always managed to maintain its heritage as a city of history and culture, has hosted many civilisations, an important region with the rich historical and cultural heritage of these civilizations. The first settlement was found in Amida Höyük in the Inner Sea in 5,000 BC. In the 3,000 BC, the fortress, which began to form with the first fortress structure of the Hurri, was shaped by the traces of 33 civilizations that ruled the city, reaching its present time in a multicultural process of multicultural formation. The Diyarbakır Castle, which began with the Hurris and then with the Assyrians and then with the Roman Empire in the 4th century C.E., became increasingly expanded and in its final form under the Islamic era, maintained its originality and its 7,000 years of historical existence, formed in line with the needs of civilizations, cultures and times in the region. Diyarbakir, a major stop on major trade routes throughout history, is an important city in the Roman era, while the capital of the Islamic states Marwani, Artuklu and Akkoyulu also served as capitals during the period. The castle, which has witnessed many wars in history, has survived with repairs and additions, and the documenting of each action on the castle has transformed the castle into a museum of inscriptions. Diyarbakır Castle surrounds the city with 7,000 years of history. The entire Surici region in Sur district of Diyarbakır was declared an urban site in 1988, followed by the list of the World Cultural Heritage Heritage Site of Diyarbakır Castle and Hevsel Gardens in July 2015. There are a total of 602 registered structures in Tyre, which are 454 (454) civilian structures, a monumental structure, and there is a very serious cultural presence when other complements of urban tissue are added to them. They include 40 mosques and mosques; 19 visits, shrines, ready, 11 churches and 4 madrases. The curfew has been declared in Sur district five times before. Many historical buildings and places of worship in the district have been damaged, according to reports and media reports from the review committees heading to the region shortly after these bans were lifted. Some of the damaged historical sites include the Mosque of Lead, the Four-legged Minare, the Paşa bath, the Hasırlı People's Assembly, the Yogurtian Market, the Surp Giragos Armenian Church, the Catholic Armenian Church, Hadji Hamit Cami and Dengbej House. In the 6th curfew, which entered its eighty-fourth day, historical structures are not known for their absence. According to media reports only to the media, memorial and civilian architecture have been severely damaged. The previous ban shows that the Leady Mosque, where the destruction was most common, was burned, but yet it was known that it was still in place, while the last footage of it was reflected in the press showed that it had also been destroyed. There is a risk that the damaged structures in the region will be removed from the UNESCO list. If curfews continue and historical structures cannot be prevented from destruction, there is a very good chance that Surici will be removed from UNESCO's list of "World Cultural Heritagees" and transferred to the World Heritage List Under Danger. The curfew in Sur district of the city, which hosted thousands of years of civilization, has been banned and tried to destroy not only the lives of the people in the region, but also its history, its culture. A commission must be set up to determine the destruction of historical and monument structures, places of worship in Sur district during the eighty-four-day curfew.
